\DIFdelbegin \subsection{\DIFdel{In-Distribution Performance}}

%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{subsection}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \subsection{\DIFadd{Time Order and Benign Sessions}}
\label{sec:res:drift}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Before any transfer question can be posed, the in-distribution number has to be pinned to a protocol, because the protocol moves it more than the architecture does. Table~\ref{tab:indist} reports macro-$F_{1}$ on }%DIFDELCMD < \DA{} %%%
\DIFdel{under two protocols that differ only in how the split is drawn: a random split over flow records, which is what most of this literature reports, and a run-disjoint split in which whole capture runs go to exactly one side. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The radio layer spans \DriftDays{} days, but its capture order follows the scenario schedule (Table~\ref{tab:benign}). Nine benign sessions were recorded on days 0 to 6, the five attack categories in blocks on days 47 to 53, and one last benign session on day 53. Every radio label is a session label: a session is benign, or one attack category, throughout. A split on session order therefore holds out whole attack categories. At training fractions of 0.4 to 0.7, the latest sessions contain \DvAuditUnseenMin{} to \DvAuditUnseenMax{} attack categories absent from training, and a single benign session. Such a split measures the detection of unseen categories, so we do not report it as drift.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{To hold category coverage fixed, we held out the latest session of every category, benign included, and compared the result with \DvNctrl{} draws that hold out a random session of every category (Table~\ref{tab:timesplit}). With the latest sessions held out, balanced accuracy is \DvFwdBAmin{} to \DvFwdBAmax{}, against control means of \DvCtrlBAmin{} to \DvCtrlBAmax{}, yet \DvFwdPctMin\% to \DvFwdPctMax\% of control draws score as low. With the earliest sessions held out, it is \DvRevBAmin{} to \DvRevBAmax{}, which \DvRevPctMin\% to \DvRevPctMax\% of control draws match or exceed. Time order does not move the result outside ordinary session-to-session variation.

}

\DIFaddend The \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{difference is large and itis not a constant offset. It spans }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{+0.09} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{+0.17} %%%
\DIFdel{of macro-$F_{1}$, and it \emph{tracks model flexibility}: a single depth-12 tree gains most , the boosted ensembles next, the linear model least. The two trivial baselines gain nothing at all, and that is the control which identifies the mechanism. A classifier with no capacity to memorise cannot profit from a leaky split, so the gain is memorisation rather than a change in task difficulty. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The consequence for a reader of this literature is direct: a published random-split score is inflated \emph{most} for exactly the flexible architectures most likely to be published, so the inflation cannot be mentally subtracted as a fixed correction}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{benign session in the test set decides it}\DIFaddend . \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Held out one at a time (Table~\ref{tab:benign}), \BenLowN{} of the ten benign sessions draw a false positive rate of at most \BenLowMax{}, while sessions \BenHighSessions{} (days \BenHighDays{}) draw \BenHighMin{} to \BenHighMax{}. The last benign session, the only one recorded during the attack period, draws \BenLateFPR{}, or \BenLateFA{} false alerts per benign UE-hour, but two sessions from the first week draw as much. With ten benign sessions, a change over time cannot be separated from this heterogeneity. The practical consequence is that a detector's false positive rate on a new site cannot be read off its rate on the benign sessions it was tested on.

}\DIFaddend

\subsection{\DIFadd{The Target Corpus: Labels and a Published Result}}
\label{sec:res:target}

\DIFadd{\emph{Label conflicts.} Before transferring }\DIFaddend to \DB{}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. Every non-trivial architecture degrades, and all six differences survive Holm correction across the family. That is the less informative half of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, we asked whether its labels are a function of its flow records. For half of the corpus they are not. Of the \NDbFlows{} flows, \LcShareAll\% (\LcFlowsAll) share every field except the row index, \texttt{Seq} and \texttt{Offset} with a flow of the opposite label (Table~\ref{tab:conflict}). All of them lie in the two UDP-flood captures, \LcBenignFileConf\% of file~\LcBenignFile{} and \LcAttackFileConf\% of file~\LcAttackFile{}, one from each base station. The pattern is specific: each of the \LcTwinAttack{} flows labeled UDP flood in file~\LcAttackFile{} has an identical copy labeled benign in file~\LcBenignFile{}, in equal numbers for \LcTwinEqual{} of }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{result. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The informative half is the floor. A macro-$F_{1}$ of $0.53$ carries no meaning until one knows what guessing scores on the same corpus, and on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\LcTwinRecords{} distinct records. The same traffic appears to have been captured at both stations and labeled differently at each; the copies make up \LcBenignTwinPct\% of the corpus's benign class. We cannot tell from the published files which label is correct, so every }\DIFaddend \DB{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a stratified random classifier scores }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.4240}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{result below is given with and without these benign copies, and no label is changed.

}

\DIFadd{The conflicts set a ceiling on every classifier that reads the record}\DIFaddend . The best \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{architecture clears that by }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.148}%%%
\DIFdel{; the MLP scores }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.4223}%%%
\DIFdel{, which is \emph{beneath} the floor, with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{any function of the \LcNFeat{} content fields can do, even evaluated on the flows it was built from, is }\DIFaddend a balanced accuracy of \DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.4966} %%%
\DIFdel{--- on the wrong side of chance. Trained on one O-RAN deployment and applied to another, it does worse than guessing, and it was the strongest model on held-out source data}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\LcCeilNative{}; in the 18 shared columns the ceiling is \LcCeilShared{}, which is where DT, XGB, HGB and the MLP sit when trained and tested on a random split of }\DB{} \DIFadd{(Table~\ref{tab:transfer_rev}). With \texttt{Seq} and \texttt{Offset} added, every record becomes unique and the ceiling is \LcCeilPos{}}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{That last observation generalises. The architecture ranked first on the source ranks }\DIFdelend \emph{\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{last of six}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{References inside the target.}\DIFaddend } \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{on the target, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{To separate what transfer costs from what }\DB{} \DIFadd{itself allows, we trained and tested the six architectures on }\DB{} \DIFadd{alone (Table~\ref{tab:target_ref}). On a random split they reach \TgtRandShBAmin{} to \TgtRandShBAmax{} in the shared space, the ceiling above, and \TgtRandShCleanBAmin{} to \TgtRandShCleanBAmax{} when the benign copies are left out of the test set. On held-out capture files they reach \TgtFileShBAmin{} to \TgtFileShBAmax{} in the shared space }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{across the six the rank correlation between in-distribution and transfer performance is not significant (Spearman $\rho = \measured{0.143}$}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\TgtFileNatBAmin{} to \TgtFileNatBAmax{} with native features; none of the three draws held out a UDP-flood capture. Across the two base stations, within one corpus and one exporter}\DIFaddend , \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$p = \measured{0.787}$). A practitioner selecting an architecture by its published in-distribution score therefore has close to no information about how it will transfer --- a stronger statement than the usual observation that scores drop.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{balanced accuracy falls to \TgtSiteABShBAmin{} to \TgtSiteABShBAmax{} from station 1 to station 2 and \TgtSiteBAShBAmin{} to \TgtSiteBAShBAmax{} in the other direction. Both failures come from the copies: trained on station 1, whose labels call the flood copies benign, a model misses station 2's floods; trained on station 2, it flags station 1's benign-labeled copies, and without them in the test set it scores \TgtSiteBAShCleanBAmin{} to \TgtSiteBAShCleanBAmax. On flows whose label the record determines, then, the shared space separates }\DB{} \DIFadd{almost perfectly, and the target is not what limits transfer.

}\DIFaddend

Capture-file draws hold out four of the 20 files.}

\DIFadd{\emph{A published result down the ladder.} The dataset's authors report binary accuracy of 99.85\% to 99.95\% on a random 70/30 split~\cite{samarakoon2022niddarxiv}. We reproduced their pipeline: the published one-hot encoding, the ten features with the highest ANOVA $F$-score, $z$-scoring, and their five classifiers with scikit-learn defaults, since the tuned values are not reported. Our $F$-scores rank the features in exactly their order, and on their split we obtain \PubRzAccMin\% to \PubRzAccMax\% accuracy for DT, RF, KNN and the MLP (Naive Bayes \PubRzAccNB\%), matching their results (Table~\ref{tab:ladder}). Their first and second features are \texttt{Seq} and \texttt{Offset}, a record's position in its capture file, which no deployed detector can observe and which, by the ceilings above, is what separates the conflicting records. Replacing only these two with the next two features of their ranking drops DT, RF and the MLP to \PubOneNoposAccMin\% to \PubOneNoposAccMax\% accuracy (balanced accuracy \PubOneNoposBAmin{} to \PubOneNoposBAmax{}, false positive rate \PubOneNoposFPRmin{} to \PubOneNoposFPRmax). The drop is the label conflict, not a loss of attack signal: on the test flows without the benign copies, RF still scores \PubOneRFclean{}. The two counters let the published models tell identical records apart by where they sit in their file. Held out by capture file, the published features average \PubTwoPubBAmin{} to \PubTwoPubBAmax{} for DT, RF and the MLP, with RF ranging from \PubTwoPubFoldMin{} to \PubTwoPubFoldMax{} across folds; without the counters the average is \PubTwoNoposBAmin{} to \PubTwoNoposBAmax{} and RF ranges from \PubTwoNoposFoldMin{} to \PubTwoNoposFoldMax{}, with accuracy as low as \PubTwoNoposFoldAccMin\% on the fold that tests one flood capture after training on the other. Across base stations the published features give \PubThreePubBAmin{} to \PubThreePubBAmax{}, and the features without counters \PubThreeNoposBAmin{} to \PubThreeNoposBAmax{}. A published 99.9\% on this corpus therefore says little about a new capture file or a new base station, and nothing about a deployment, where record positions do not exist.
}\DIFaddend \begin{table}[t]

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{How separable are the two corpora in the shared space? We do not answer this with a covariate-shift statistic of our own, because a stronger answer already exists: Abraheem and Edhirig~\cite{abraheem2026bidirectional} train a classifier to predict \emph{which corpus} a flow came from and reach a balanced accuracy of 0.993 on harmonised featuresof the same two corpora, a figure that survives correlation alignment and feature filtering. The corpora are trivially separable. That bounds what any transfer measurement between them can mean, and it is the quantitative form of the exporterconfound declared in Section~\ref{sec:setup}.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \end{table*}
\subsection{\DIFadd{Transfer Across Corpora}}
\label{sec:res:transfer}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The reverse direction, }%DIFDELCMD < \DB{} %%%
\DIFdel{to }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Table~\ref{tab:transfer} and Fig.~\ref{fig:transfer} report transfer from }\DIFaddend \DA{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, is reported separately as a symmetry check rather than as a second headline. It distinguishes ``detectors do not transfer'' from ``}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{to }\DB{}\DIFadd{. In macro-$F_1$ every supervised model loses \TrFoneDropMin{} to \TrFoneDropMax{}, but the majority baseline, which reads no features, loses \TrMajDrop{} as well, because }\DIFaddend \DB{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is simply harder'', and it carries a caveat the forward directiondoes not: }%DIFDELCMD < \DB{} %%%
\DIFdel{publishes no identifiers, so it has no group key, and its own held-out reference is a random split and therefore an optimistic bound. Its $\Delta_{F_{1}}$ is consequently an over-estimate.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{We do not report a fine-tuning result. Few-shot adaptation on labelled target data is a different experiment, it is already published for these corpora~\cite{abraheem2026bidirectional}, and it relocates the problem rather than solving it : it means each deployment requires its own labelled attack data, which is exactly the cost a pre-trained detection xApp was supposed to avoid. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The degradation is statistically unambiguous. A paired $t$-test over }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{20} %%%
\DIFdel{split seeds rejects the null hypothesis of equal held-out and cross-deployment }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{has a lower attack prevalence than }\DA{}\DIFadd{. Measured against that baseline the }\DIFaddend macro-\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$F_{1}$ for **all six** non-trivial architectures, and every one survives Holm correction across the family ($p_{\text{Holm}} \le \measured{0.0069}$). Effect sizes are large, with $d_{z}$ }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{$F_1$ loss is between \TrDiDmin{} and \TrDiDmax{}, and after correction none of the macro-$F_1$ losses is significant. Macro-$F_1$ is the wrong instrument here. Balanced accuracy, which prevalence cannot move, shows the loss clearly: from \TrSrcBAmin{}--\TrSrcBAmax{} on held-out source data to \TrTgtBAmin{}--\TrTgtBAmax{} on }\DB{}\DIFadd{, a drop of \TrBAdropMin{} to \TrBAdropMax{} that is significant for \TrSigBA{} of six architectures after correction, is positive in at least \TrBAseedsMin{} of 20 splits for every architecture, and averages \TrBAdropAvg{} }[\DIFadd{\TrBAdropAvgNbLo, \TrBAdropAvgNbHi}]\DIFadd{. The floors do not move. Target ROC-AUC lies }\DIFaddend between \DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.68} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.86}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\TrTgtAUCmin{} and \TrTgtAUCmax{}}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The choice among detector families is a second-order effect beside the shared failure to transfer, and this is the more useful way to read the table: the spread across architectures on the target, }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.149} %%%
\DIFdel{of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The detectors remain above chance on the target, but not far above it: target balanced accuracy averages \TrTgtBAavg{}, and for \TrChanceSig{} of six architectures its corrected interval excludes 0.5. For reference, the stratified sampler scores \TrStratFloor{} }\DIFaddend macro-\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$F_{1}$ from best to worst, is smaller than the distance any of them has fallen, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{$F_1$ on }\DB{} \DIFadd{and a fair coin would score \TrFairCoin{}; we report both because the value of a macro-$F_1$ floor depends on which prior it samples. LR transfers best by every prevalence-free measure (balanced accuracy \TrLRtgtBA{}, ROC-AUC \TrLRtgtAUC{}); the MLP, best on held-out source balanced accuracy (\TrMLPsrcBA{}), reaches \TrMLPtgtBA{} on the target. Source performance does not predict target performance in a prevalence-free metric: across the six architectures the rank correlation is \RhoBA{} in balanced accuracy ($p=\RhoBAP$). In macro-$F_1$ it is \RhoFone{} ($p=\RhoFoneP$), but macro-$F_1$ ranks move with prevalence, }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the ordering is not the source ordering.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{six architectures give neither value much precision.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A single aggregate score conceals the shape of the failure, so Table~\ref{tab:percat} reports detection per attack category on the target. The benign column is specificity; the remainder are recall.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\textbf{No architecture is usable on both classes.} Logistic regression holds }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.977} %%%
\DIFdel{benign specificity and detects }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.247} %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{The target's labels.} Part of this loss is the label conflict of Section~\ref{sec:res:target}. Detectors that learned a UDP flood on }\DA{} \DIFadd{detect the floods of capture file~\LcAttackFile{} and, by the same rule, flag between \NetCopyFlagMin{} and \NetCopyFlagMax{} of the benign-labeled copies in file~\LcBenignFile{}; LR detects neither. Evaluated without the copies, on a deterministic re-run of 10 }\DIFaddend of the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{denial-of-service traffic. The MLP detects }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.884} %%%
\DIFdel{of it, and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.807} %%%
\DIFdel{of the scanning traffic, while classifying }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.885} %%%
\DIFdel{of benign traffic as attack --- which is why its macro-$F_1$ falls beneath the trivial floor. The tree ensembles occupy a middle position and are the weakest at scanning, recovering }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.168} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.295} %%%
\DIFdel{of it. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{20 split seeds, target balanced accuracy is \TrCleanTgtBAmin{} to \TrCleanTgtBAmax{}: \TrCleanOthMin{} to \TrCleanOthMax{} for the five nonlinear architectures and \TrCleanLR{} for LR. The loss from held-out source data shrinks to \TrCleanDropMin{} to \TrCleanDropMax{} and is significant for \TrCleanSig{} of six architectures after correction. The conflicts therefore reverse which detector looks transferable: on all flows LR looks best, on flows whose label the record determines it looks worst. Against the target's own reference on those flows (Table~\ref{tab:target_ref}), transfer still falls short by \TrCleanShortMin{} to \TrCleanShortMax{} of balanced accuracy.

}\DIFaddend

\DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \subsection{%%%
\DIFdel{Score Calibration and Prior Shift}%DIFDELCMD < \MBLOCKRIGHTBRACE
%DIFDELCMD < \label{sec:calibration}
%DIFDELCMD < 
%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Per-category detection (Table~\ref{tab:percat}) shows that no architecture is usable on both classes of }\DB{}\DIFadd{: LR keeps a benign specificity of \FwdLRspec{} and detects \FwdLRdos{} of DoS traffic, while the tree models and the MLP detect more attacks at the price of flagging \FwdEnsFlagMin{} to \FwdEnsFlagMax{} of benign traffic. In the reverse direction, categories absent from }\DB{} \DIFadd{are not detected uniformly: the ensembles and the MLP recall at most \RevWebEnsMax{} of web attacks, whereas brute-force attacks, also absent, are recalled at \RevBFmin{} to \RevBFmax.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{All three deployment criteria depend on the score $\hat{p}$ behaving as a probability, because \eqref{eq:ppv} and \eqref{eq:tau} both operate on a threshold applied to it.We therefore report expected calibration error over fifteen \emph{equal-mass} bins; equal-width bins are misleading here, because scores pile up near 1.0 and most equal-width bins are then empty. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{In distribution the models are reasonably calibrated, with ECE between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.028} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.185}%%%
\DIFdel{. Under transfer they are not: target ECE rises to between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.251} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.396}%%%
\DIFdel{. That is the expected finding, and it suggested an obvious remedy --- calibrate on held-out source data and recover the operating point. We tested it, and the remedy fails in an instructive way. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \begin{table*}[t]
\caption{Per-category detection, mean over 20 split seeds at $\tau=0.5$. ``Ben.'' is specificity; other columns are recall. BF: brute force. Categories absent from \DB{} can be tested only in the reverse direction.}
\label{tab:percat}

Model & Ben. & DoS & Probe & Ben. & DoS & Probe & DDoS & BF & Web \\
\midrule
LR & 0.98 & 0.25 & 0.64 & 0.71 & 0.85 & 0.92 & 0.96 & 0.57 & 0.30 \\

\DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{Fitting Platt scaling or isotonic regression on a group-disjoint source calibration fold halves both Brier score and ECE, exactly as advertised, and simultaneously makes the operating point far worse. For logistic regression on the source , Brier falls from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.1023} %%%
\DIFdelFL{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0486} %%%
\DIFdelFL{while deployment PPV falls from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0174} %%%
\DIFdelFL{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0025} %%%
\DIFdelFL{and the alert queue grows from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{35{,}330} %%%
\DIFdelFL{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{216{,}846} %%%
\DIFdelFL{per hour. A calibrator fitted on a corpus that is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{94.63}%%%
\DIFdelFL{\% attack learns to map scores onto that prior, so applied anywhere it pushes almost every score past $\tau=0.5$ and the detector flags nearly everything.

}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \end{table*}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{There is a stronger statement available, and it explains why no calibrator could have worked. Every method tested here is a monotone transform, so none changes the model's ranking of examples and none changes the set of achievable operating points. We verify this rather than assert it: Platt and temperature scaling preserve ROC-AUC to within $10^{-5}$ for five of six architectures. **Threshold saturation is therefore not a calibration failure but a discriminability limit. ** Target ROC-AUC lies between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.537} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.691}%%%
\DIFdel{, and no threshold, under any calibration, extracts a usable operating point from a curve that close to the diagonal.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \subsection{\DIFadd{What the Transfer Gap Is Made Of}}
\label{sec:res:anatomy}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{One methodological warning follows, because itnearly produced a positive result from nothing. Temperature scaling appears in our sweep to rescue two architectures that raw scores cannot rescue. It preserves the ROC exactly, so it rescues nothing: it rescales the scores so that a \emph{fixed grid} of swept thresholds lands on a different part of the same curve. Any comparison of monotone calibrators over a fixed threshold grid can manufacture this artefact, and the ROC should be reported alongside the grid. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{This compounds the earlier findings rather than merely adding to them. The remedy suggested by Fig.~\ref{fig:alerts} --- raising the threshold to suppress alert volume --- assumes that a high score means high confidence, and under transfer it does not, so the operating point chosen on the source deployment does not carry over even in the ordering it induces.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{A second and independent effect is prior shift, and it is severe. Training prevalence on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Separability.} A classifier trained to tell a }\DA{} \DIFadd{flow from a }\DB{} \DIFadd{flow reaches \DomShared{} balanced accuracy on the 18 shared columns and \DomRobust{} on the nine exporter-robust columns alone. The largest per-feature shifts are the protocol mix (most }\DIFaddend \DA{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{94.63}%%%
\DIFdel{\%, target prevalenceon }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{flows are TCP and most }\DIFaddend \DB{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{60.71}%%%
\DIFdel{\%, and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{flows UDP), }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{operational prevalence assumed in Section~\ref{sec:setup} is $\pi=0.002$. A classifier trained under one prior emits scores calibrated to that prior. Where the training prior $\pi_{\text{tr}}$ is known, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{direction ratios and }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{scores admit a closed-form correction, }%DIFDELCMD < \begin{equation}
%DIFDELCMD < \tilde{p} \;=\; \frac{\hat{p}\,\dfrac{\pi}{\pi_{\text{tr}}}}{\hat{p}\,\dfrac{\pi}{\pi_{\text{tr}}} \;+\; \left(1-\hat{p}\right)\dfrac{1-\pi}{1-\pi_{\text{tr}}}},
%DIFDELCMD < \label{eq:prior}
%DIFDELCMD < \end{equation}
%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{which rescales the decision surface without retraining.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{packet counts, and the robust columns shift as much as the segmentation-dependent ones ($\bar{W}_1$ of \WoneRobust{} against \WoneNonrobust). The corpora therefore differ in traffic composition, which no choice of exporter would remove, as well as in flow segmentation, which a common exporter would.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{We tested this correction and it does not work either, which is worth reporting because it is the obvious next thing to try. Applying \eqref{eq:prior} requires the target prior, which a deployment does not know; we supply the true value anyway, as an oracle, to bound what the correction could achieve. Even so it does not beat leaving the scores alone: for logistic regression, oracle prior correction gives an operational precision of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0197} %%%
\DIFdel{against }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0221} %%%
\DIFdel{for raw scores. It does cut the alert queue sharply, from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{19{,}551} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{3{,}285} %%%
\DIFdel{per hour, by moving to a far more conservative operating point, and it pays for that with more than half the remaining recall. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Model families and adaptation.} Table~\ref{tab:arms} evaluates the same splits under three changes, and none produces a transferable detector. Restricting the detectors to the nine exporter-robust columns raises held-out source balanced accuracy to \RobSrcBAmin{} to \RobSrcBAmax{}, yet target balanced accuracy falls to \RobBAmin{} to \RobBAmax{}, no better than chance: the columns that do not depend on flow segmentation carry the traffic-composition shift measured above. CORAL, which reads unlabeled target features, gives target balanced accuracy of \CoralBAmin{} to \CoralBAmax{}; only the MLP gains (\CoralMLPtgtBA{} against \TrMLPtgtBA{} without adaptation), and the target false positive rate rises to \CoralFPRmin{} to \CoralFPRmax{}. The benign-only novelty detectors fail already on held-out source data: in this flow space, floods and scans resemble short benign flows.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Prior shift is therefore real, and correcting it helps the queue rather than the precision. It is not the binding constraint}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Cost of the shared space.} Projecting }\DA{} \DIFadd{onto the 18 shared columns costs between \HarmBAmin{} and \HarmBAmax{} of in-distribution balanced accuracy relative to all \NFullFeat{} non-port Zeek features (Table~\ref{tab:harm}). The low source macro-$F_1$ of Table~\ref{tab:transfer} reflects the \PrevDa\% attack prevalence of }\DA{} \DIFadd{rather than weak detectors; their source balanced accuracy is \TrSrcBAmin{} to \TrSrcBAmax}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{\textbf{That claim is withdrawn.} It was registered in advance with a falsification condition, and the condition fired: the record-level join between the flow records and the radio telemetry cannot be established from the published artefacts, because the flow table carries no time column and shares no identifier with the radio layer. Without that join the ablation cannot be constructed at all, and no result we could report here would be supported by the data.

}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \end{table}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{We record the withdrawal rather than deleting the question, because the question remains a good one and because the pre-registered failure criterion is what makes the withdrawal reportable rather than embarrassing. Fard \textit{et al.} publish the in-distribution half of the effect on this same corpus; the transfer half, which is the part that would have been ours, is untested.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdelend \subsection{Alert Burden}
\DIFaddbegin \label{sec:res:alerts}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Fig.~\ref{fig:alerts} sweeps the decision threshold and reports both the resulting hourly alert volume from \eqref{eq:alerts} and the operational precision from \eqref{eq:ppv}. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The gap between the two precision figures is the result. On the corpus, precision at $\tau=0.5$ is approximately }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.93} %%%
\DIFdel{for every detector, and the six are indistinguishable from one another. Translated through \eqref{eq:ppv} at }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Table~\ref{tab:pooled} gives pooled operating points at $\tau=0.5$ on three evaluation sets, with 95\% cluster-bootstrap intervals. On the radio layer, the six detectors have a pooled window false positive rate of \RadFPRmin{} to \RadFPRmax{}, or \RadFAmin{} to \RadFAmax{} false alerts per benign UE-hour, and at }\DIFaddend $\pi=0.002$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, pooled operational precision is between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0055} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0077} %%%
\DIFdel{--- a collapse by a factor of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{132}%%%
\DIFdel{, with the detectors still indistinguishable. Alert volume runs to tens of thousands }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{an operational precision of \RadPPVmin{} to \RadPPVmax{} against a corpus precision of \RadPrecMin{} to \RadPrecMax{}. These point estimates are not stable. The false positive rate is set by which of the ten benign sessions land in the test set (Table~\ref{tab:benign}), and the bootstrap interval of the pooled rate runs from \RadCbFPRlo{} to \RadCbFPRhi{} across architectures, or \RadCbFAlo{} to \RadCbFAhi{} false alerts per benign UE-hour; operational precision at $\tau=0.5$ runs from \RadCbPPVlo{} to \RadCbPPVhi{}. Thirty sessions do not determine the radio layer's alert burden; they show only that it depends on the benign conditions a detector meets. On held-out }\DA{} \DIFadd{flows the false positive rate is \NetSrcFPRmin{} to \NetSrcFPRmax{}, which at $\lambda_b = 240{,}000$ benign flows per hour is \NetSrcFAmin{} to \NetSrcFAmax{} false alerts per hour; on }\DB{} \DIFadd{it is \NetTgtFPRmin{} to \NetTgtFPRmax{}, or \NetTgtFAmin{} to \NetTgtFAmax{} per hour. Without the benign copies of Section~\ref{sec:res:target} it is \NetCleanFPRmin{} to \NetCleanFPRmax{}, still \NetCleanFAmin{} to \NetCleanFAmax{} false alerts }\DIFaddend per hour, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the large majority false}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and operational precision is \NetCleanPPVmin{} to \NetCleanPPVmax{}}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Two properties of this collapse matter more than its size at any single operating point. First, it is not an artefact of the base rate we declared. Sweeping }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Raising the threshold helps only on held-out }\DA{} \DIFadd{flows (Fig.~\ref{fig:threshold}). The best threshold is chosen on the evaluation data, so the values below are upper bounds. With recall held above 10\%, high thresholds reach an operational precision of \NetSrcPPVbest{} there, but the best any detector reaches on }\DB{} \DIFadd{is \NetTgtPPVbest{}, and \NetCleanPPVbest{} without the copies. On the radio layer the best pooled value is \RadPPVbest{}, but its bootstrap interval reaches 1.0 for \RadCbBestHiN{} of six architectures: a resample that omits the benign sessions flagged almost entirely leaves thresholds without a single false positive. The step from corpus to operational precision is arithmetic in }\DIFaddend $\pi$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0001} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.5} %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{: sweeping $\pi$ from $10^{-4}$ to 0.5 }\DIFaddend moves the collapse factor from \DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \measured{2{,}631} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.19}%%%
\DIFdel{, and the detectors remain within a factor of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.40} %%%
\DIFdel{of one another throughout.Second, the two-dimensional parameter space is really one-dimensional: operational precision depends on $\pi$ and is invariant to the benign arrival rate, while alert volume scales linearly in that rate and independently of it . We verify both numerically rather than asserting them.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\textbf{A caution on how this quantity is estimated.} Operational precision }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\RadCollapseLowPi{} to \RadCollapseHighPi{} (Fig.~\ref{fig:prevalence}), so an operational precision means nothing without the prevalence it assumes.
\emph{How operational precision should be estimated.} PPV }\DIFaddend is strongly non-linear in the false positive rate near zero, so a \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{single evaluation }\DIFdelend fold that happens to \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{produce no false positives yields a precision of exactly one. Averaging that across folds, rather than pooling confusion counts, inflates the apparent spread between architectures: on our radio folds, which carry a median of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{136} %%%
\DIFdel{benign windows, the same data gives a spread of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.40} %%%
\DIFdel{pooledand }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{13.18} %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{contain no false positive yields $\mathrm{PPV}=1$ whatever its recall. Averaging per-fold PPV therefore rewards the architecture that draws the most such folds. On the radio layer the same counts give a spread between the best and worst architecture of \EstPooled{} when pooled, \EstMedian{} as a median over folds and \EstMean{} }\DIFaddend as a mean over folds \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. The distortion tracks the number of negatives per fold --- it is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.4} %%%
\DIFdel{on the much larger network folds and vanishes when the target corpus is evaluated whole. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{(Table~\ref{tab:estimator}). }\DIFaddend We report pooled counts throughout\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and report the mean alongside so that the size of the artefact remains visible.

An earlier version of this work reported the mean.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The cross-deployment case is considerably worse, and it separates the architectures in a way the source corpus does not. On }%DIFDELCMD < \DB{} %%%
\DIFdel{at $\tau=0.5$ the false positive rate ranges from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.023} %%%
\DIFdel{for logistic regression to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.885} %%%
\DIFdel{for the MLP. At the same benign flow rate that is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{5{,}620} %%%
\DIFdel{false alerts per hour at one end and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{212{,}383} %%%
\DIFdel{at the other, against pooled operational precisions of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0232} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0020} %%%
\DIFdel{respectively --- a factor of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{23} %%%
\DIFdel{in burden for }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.042} %%%
\DIFdel{of macro-$F_{1}$. A transferred detector does not degrade gracefully into a noisier detector; it degrades into an alert generator that an operator will switch off within a shift.

\subsection{\DIFadd{Calibration and Prior Shift}}
\label{sec:res:calibration}

\DIFadd{Under transfer the scores are poorly calibrated (Fig.~\ref{fig:reliability}). We fitted three calibrators on a group-disjoint source calibration fold (Table~\ref{tab:calibration}). Whether a calibrator can change the achievable operating points depends on whether it preserves the order of scores. Temperature scaling does, and preserves ROC-AUC to within \CalTempMax{}. Platt scaling is monotone but its fitted slope can be negative; for DT in \CalPlattFlipSeeds{} of \CalSeeds{} seeds it inverted the ranking and changed ROC-AUC by \CalPlattFlip. In that seed the tree's scores carried no ranking information on the calibration fold (ROC-AUC \PlattCalAUC{} on \PlattCalGroups{} }\DIFaddend held-out source \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{data --- pooled PPV spans a factor of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.40} %%%
\DIFdel{--- and an order of magnitude apart on the target, where the spread is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{11.68}%%%
\DIFdel{. Which corpus one evaluates on decides whether the architectures look alike or not. Second, the model that wins on the target operationally is the simplest one, and it is not the model that wins on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{addresses) while they ranked the test fold well (\PlattTestAUC), so Platt scaling fitted the fold faithfully with a slope of \PlattSlope{} and inverted the test ranking. The failure belongs to small group-disjoint calibration folds, which need not resemble the test fold, rather than to Platt scaling. Isotonic regression is monotone but piecewise constant: it merges scores into ties and, on its fitting data, yields the ROC convex hull~\cite{fawcett2007pav}; its largest change in ROC-AUC ranged from \CalIsoMin{} to \CalIsoMax{} across architectures. None of the calibrators raises target }\DIFaddend macro-\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$F_{1}$ there. Whichever single metric is chosen, it recommends a different system}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{$F_1$ above the raw scores for any architecture: fitted on a source with \PrevDa\% attacks, they move scores toward that prior and flag more benign traffic on }\DB{}\DIFaddend .

\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The obvious remedy for prior shift is to rescale scores to the target prior~\cite{saerens2002adjusting}, which a deployment does not know. Two estimators recover it from unlabeled data under the assumption that only the class balance changes: expectation maximization~\cite{saerens2002adjusting} and black-box shift estimation~\cite{lipton2018bbse}. Here that assumption fails. Against a true target prior of \PriorTrue{}, their per-architecture mean estimates range from \PriorMeanMin{} to \PriorMeanMax{}, and \PriorFracExtreme\% of individual estimates fall below 0.05 or above 0.95. Rescaling with the true prior, an oracle, does not beat the raw scores either (Table~\ref{tab:calibration}). The limit is discriminability on the target, with ROC-AUC of \TrTgtAUCmin{} to \TrTgtAUCmax{}, not calibration.

}

\centering

\subsection{\DIFadd{Decision Latency}}
\label{sec:res:latency}

%DIF >  Results: decision latency (EXP-043). Numbers are macros from
%DIF >  tables/generated/numbers.tex, computed by analysis/latency_outputs.py.
\DIFaddend Table~\ref{tab:latency} reports \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the latency decomposition of \eqref{eq:latency} at an offered load of 1}%DIFDELCMD < {%%%
\DIFdel{, }%DIFDELCMD < }%%%
\DIFdel{000 decisions per second, together with sustainable throughput under the p99 constraint of \eqref{eq:budget} and container resource consumption. Fig.~\ref{fig:latency} shows how the }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{per-call latency on one host for three implementations of each model: scikit-learn called with a one-row pandas DataFrame, which is how the model is called inside the experiment code; scikit-learn called with a NumPy array on one thread; and ONNX Runtime on one thread, used only where the exported model reproduced scikit-learn's decisions (\LatOnnxOK{} of \LatOnnxN{} exports, with decision agreement of at least \LatOnnxAgreeMin). A no-op call has a }\DIFaddend 99th percentile \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{grows with load}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{of \LatFloorPnn{}\,ms, so the platform floor does not affect the comparisons}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Three observations follow. First, median latency is a misleading summary. Four of the six architectures sit inside a 10}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The implementation moves latency more than the architecture does. For LR, the DataFrame path costs \LatLRdfPfifty{}}\DIFaddend \,ms \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{budget at $p_{50}$ and stay inside it at $p_{99}$; the two that do not --- histogram gradient boosting and the random forest --- miss it by an order of magnitude, with $p_{99}$ of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{94.62} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{378.05}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at the median against \LatLRarrPfifty{}}\DIFaddend \,\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ms for an array, the difference being input validation. RF predicting one row through its default thread pool takes \LatRFthreadsPfifty{}\,}\DIFaddend ms \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{against medians of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{53.16} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{93.41}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at the median, \LatRFarrPfifty{}}\DIFaddend \,\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ms on one thread and \LatRFonnxPfifty{}\,}\DIFaddend ms \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. The tail, not the median, decides conformance. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{Second, the verdict depends on where the budget is drawn, and O-RAN specifies the near-real-time loop over a range spanning two orders of magnitude . At 1}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{in ONNX Runtime. Across architectures, the 99th percentile of ONNX Runtime lies between \LatOnnxPnnMin{} and \LatOnnxPnnMax{}}\DIFaddend \,\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{ms nothing conforms; at 5}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ms on the radio layer, against up to \LatArrPnnMax{}}\DIFaddend \,\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{ms the decision tree alone; at 10}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ms for scikit-learn with arrays. A ranking of architectures by latency is therefore a ranking of implementations, and a comparison with measurements taken on other hosts or in other languages~\cite{obiuwevwi2026realtime} says nothing about the architectures. For context only, Obiuwevwi \emph{et al.} measure inference inside an OpenAirInterface and FlexRIC near-real-time RIC in the microsecond range for logistic regression and a small MLP~\cite{obiuwevwi2026realtime}, within an order of magnitude of our ONNX Runtime figures.

}

\DIFadd{For a window-level detector the decision path is window aggregation followed by inference. Aggregating one 16-record window costs \LatAggPnn{}}\DIFaddend \,ms \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at the 99th percentile, and }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{tree, logistic regression, XGBoost and the MLP; at 100}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{upper bound of the 99th percentile of aggregation plus ONNX inference is at most \LatEtoEPnnHiMax{}}\DIFaddend \,ms \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{all but the random forest; at 1\,s everything. Asserting a single budget converts an authoring choice into an apparent measurement, so we report the crossing point per architecture instead. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{across the architectures with a verified export. On this host that path would meet any budget in the specified range. Whether a deployment does is decided by the terms we did not measure: indication decoding, control encoding and queueing inside a RIC.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Third, and least expected, inference is not the bottleneck. Packet-level feature extraction costs a median }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{6.90}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{For a flow-level detector, features cost more than inference. On the three DDoS captures of }\DA{}\DIFadd{, the vectorized exporter produces flow records at \LatExtrMin{} to \LatExtrMax{}}\DIFaddend \,ms per flow\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{against }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.18} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.96}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, \LatExtrSpeedMin{} to \LatExtrSpeedMax{} times faster than the per-packet reference implementation on the same captures (\LatExtrRefMin{} to \LatExtrRefMax{}}\DIFaddend \,\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{ms of inference for the four architectures light enough to meet a 10\,}\DIFdelend ms \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{budget. We re-implemented the exporter with bulk array extraction and measured a speed-up of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{2.3} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{12.1} %%%
\DIFdel{times, verified to produce identical flow records; extraction still costs }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.058}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{per flow); per-flow cost grows with packets per flow. On the \LatEqCaptures{} captures compared record by record, the two exporters agree exactly in packets and bytes on all \LatEqKeys{} shared IPv4 flow keys; the reference additionally emits \LatRefOnlyMin{} and \LatRefOnlyMax{} IPv6 link-local keys, which the vectorized exporter, being IPv4-only, does not parse. Mapping one exported record onto the shared space through the pandas code of our pipeline costs a further \LatMapPfifty{}}\DIFaddend \,ms \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{per flow at best. Set against a deployed implementation rather than a prototype --- }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1}%%%
\DIFdel{--}%DIFDELCMD < \measured{5}%%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at the median, while ONNX inference on flow features costs at most \LatNetOnnxPnnMax{}}\DIFaddend \,\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$\mu$s for logistic regression inside a real Near-RT RIC~\cite{obiuwevwi2026realtime} --- extraction is not merely the larger half of the budget but essentially the whole of it. Optimisation effort directed at model compression addresses the smaller part of the problem, and the ranking of architectures by prototype latency is a statement about the prototype's toolchain rather than about the architectures}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ms at the 99th percentile. These figures describe a Python pipeline on one host: they locate the cost in feature construction, and say nothing about what a native exporter at the user plane would cost}\DIFaddend .

\section{\DIFdel{Measured Results}}
%DIFAUXCMD

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{This section reports measurements taken on the radio-telemetry layer of
}%DIFDELCMD < \DA{} %%%
\DIFdel{under the run-disjoint protocol of Section~\ref{sec:setup}. Unless
stated otherwise every figure is a mean over }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{20} %%%
\DIFdel{split seeds, with intervals given as paired $t$-intervals over split means. Model
seeds are nested inside split seeds and averaged before testing, so the effective sample size is the number of splits, not the number of fits}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{This section draws the consequences for O-RAN practice, applies the deployability test, and relates the results to the reported state of the art}\DIFaddend .

\subsection{\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Evaluation Protocol Effects}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Implications for O-RAN Practice}\DIFaddend }
\DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \label{sec:measured:leakage}
%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \label{sec:disc:practice}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{We first quantify how much of a reported score is attributable to the split protocol rather than to the detector. The same models, features and seeds are evaluated twice: once under a random split over windows, the
convention in much of the intrusion-detection literature, and once under a split in which whole capture runs are held out.}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \begin{table}[t]
%DIFDELCMD < \caption{Effect of the split protocol on macro-$F_{1}$, \DA{} radio layer.
%DIFDELCMD < Differences are paired over \measured{20} split seeds; $^{\ast}$ marks
%DIFDELCMD < significance after Holm correction within the family of eight models.}
%DIFDELCMD < \label{tab:m_leakage}
%DIFDELCMD < \centering
%DIFDELCMD < %%%
%DIF <  GENERATED by analysis/make_tables.py on 2026-09-21
%DIF <  source: results\EXP-002\processed\leakage_summary.csv
%DIF <  commit: e45aa5b
%DIF <  DO NOT EDIT. Edit the generator, then re-run `make tables`.
%DIFDELCMD < \begin{tabular}{lcccc}
%DIFDELCMD < \toprule
%DIFDELCMD < Model & Random & Run-disjoint & $\Delta$ & 95\,\% CI \\
%DIFDELCMD < \midrule
%DIFDELCMD < rf & 0.970 & 0.853 & +0.117$^{\ast}$ & [+0.057, +0.177] \\
%DIFDELCMD < mlp & 0.959 & 0.849 & +0.110$^{\ast}$ & [+0.056, +0.164] \\
%DIFDELCMD < hgb & 0.982 & 0.844 & +0.138$^{\ast}$ & [+0.082, +0.194] \\
%DIFDELCMD < xgboost & 0.977 & 0.837 & +0.140$^{\ast}$ & [+0.084, +0.195] \\
%DIFDELCMD < tree & 0.950 & 0.782 & +0.168$^{\ast}$ & [+0.110, +0.226] \\
%DIFDELCMD < logreg & 0.836 & 0.744 & +0.093$^{\ast}$ & [+0.037, +0.149] \\
%DIFDELCMD < stratified & 0.498 & 0.507 & -0.009$^{\ast}$ & [-0.011, -0.007] \\
%DIFDELCMD < majority & 0.431 & 0.434 & -0.002 & [-0.008, +0.003] \\
%DIFDELCMD < \bottomrule
%DIFDELCMD < \end{tabular}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \end{table}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{Table~\ref{tab:m_leakage} and Fig.~\ref{fig:m_leakage} show the result. Every
non-trivial architecture scores materially higher under the random split, and all six differences survive Holm correction.

The effect is not small :
it ranges from }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{9.3} %%%
\DIFdel{to }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{16.8} %%%
\DIFdel{points of macro-$F_{1}$,
with paired effect sizes $d_{z}$ between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.78} %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Data path.} E2SM-KPM delivers aggregated measurements per reporting period; it does not carry packets. A window-level detector can therefore run inside the RIC on KPM reports, but a flow-level detector needs a flow exporter next to the user plane, in the central unit or at the user-plane function, }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.35}%%%
\DIFdel{.
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{a transport of its records to the xApp, for example through a custom E2 service model or the non-real-time data collection path. That transport adds to $t_{\mathrm{ind}}$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:latency}, which we did not measure.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Two features of the pattern matter more than its magnitude. First, the
ordering tracks model flexibility. A single depth-12 decision tree gains
most, the boosted ensembles next, the random forest and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Detection delay.} Compute latency is a small part of }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{multilayer
perceptron less, and logistic regression least. Second, the two trivial
baselines gain nothing: the majority-class classifier shows
$\Delta=\measured{-0.002}$ with $p=\measured{0.40}$. A classifier with no
capacity to memorise cannot profit from a leaky split, which is what
identifies the mechanism as memorisation rather than as a change in task
difficulty. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The practical consequence is that the inflation cannot be treated as a
constant offset. It is largest for precisely the flexible architectures
that are most often reported , so a reader cannot mentally discount a
published random-split figure by a fixed amount}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{time to detect. A window-level decision cannot precede the end of its window (16\,s here), and a flow-level decision cannot precede the export of the flow record, which for a long connection waits for an idle or active timeout. The KPM reporting period and the flow timeouts should be reported beside compute latency}\DIFaddend .

%DIFDELCMD < \centering
%DIFDELCMD < \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{../figures/generated/fig_leakage_radio.pdf}
%DIFDELCMD < \caption{Macro-$F_{1}$ under a random split and a run-disjoint split.
%DIFDELCMD < Bars are means over \measured{20} split seeds.}
%DIFDELCMD < \label{fig:m_leakage}
%DIFDELCMD < \end{figure}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\subsection{\DIFdel{Operational Alert Burden}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{subsection}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < \label{sec:measured:alerts}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{Criterion 2 asks what the detector costs an operator rather than how itscores on a corpus. Table~\ref{tab:m_alerts} reports both quantities side by
side at the default threshold, under the declared deployment parameters
$\pi=0.002$ and $\lambda_{b}=240{,}000$ benign flows per hour}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Acting on alerts.} An alert that drives an E2SM-RC action, such as isolating a UE or steering it, acts on a false alert as readily as on a true one. At the false positive rates of Table~\ref{tab:pooled}, automatic mitigation on the radio layer would act against each benign UE \RadFAmin{} to \RadFAmax{} times per hour; alerts of this quality can inform an operator or a non-real-time policy through A1, but should not trigger automatic control}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \begin{table}[t]
%DIFDELCMD < \caption{Corpus precision against operational precision, \DA{} radio

\DIFdel{The two precision columns are the criterion in one line. Corpus precision
sits between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.926} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.933} %%%
\DIFdel{for all five detectors,
a spread of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.007}%%%
\DIFdel{.Once a realistic base rate is applied, the
operational precision of the same five spans }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.036} %%%
\DIFdel{to
}%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.218}%%%
\DIFdel{, a factor of six. The metric a paper would report cannot
distinguish these detectors; the metric an operator lives with separates
them sharply. Absolute volumes are }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{53{,}000} %%%
\DIFdel{to
}%DIFDELCMD < \measured{63{,}000} %%%
\DIFdel{alerts per hour, the large majority of them false}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Admission and monitoring.} The deployability test of Eq.~\eqref{eq:deploy} can serve as an admission check before an xApp is enabled on a new site, provided that site supplies labeled benign and attack traffic. After admission, unlabeled shift detection~\cite{rabanser2019failing} can flag when the incoming traffic has moved away from the training distribution; the corpora here are separable at \DomShared{} balanced accuracy, so such a monitor would fire immediately}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{None of this is a new statistical result.The base-rate argument is Axelsson's, and it is a quarter of a century old. What is new is its
quantification for a specific detector under a protocol that holds capture
runs out, reported alongside the tail-latency measurement for the same
model artefacts. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \subsection{\DIFadd{Applying the Deployability Test}}
\label{sec:disc:predicate}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A second and less expected finding concerns the operator's only runtime
control. Sweeping the decision threshold to recover precision,
Table~\ref{tab:m_ppvcross} shows that $\text{PPV}=0.5$ is
\emph{unreachable at any threshold} for four of the five detectors. The
scores of the boosted models and the perceptron saturate near unity under
a run-disjoint split, so raising $\tau$ barely moves the alert volume: the
histogram-boosted ensemble still emits }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{53{,}632} %%%
\DIFdelend %DIF >  Discussion: applying the deployability test of Eq. (7). Numbers are macros
%DIF >  from tables/generated/numbers.tex; the table is generated (EXP-052).
\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{We instantiate Eq.~\eqref{eq:deploy} with bounds an operator can defend. The generalization bound is $\delta = 0.10$ of balanced accuracy, applied to the upper end of the corrected 95\% interval. The alert term asks for a threshold that detects at least half of the attacks ($r_{\min} = 0.5$), makes at least one alert in ten a true one ($\rho = 0.1$ at $\pi = 0.002$), and raises at most $A_{\max} = 200$ false }\DIFaddend alerts per hour at \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$\tau=\measured{0.99}$. Only the random forest and the linear model
reach $\text{PPV}=0.5$, and both do so by collapsing recall, to
}%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.418} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.189} %%%
\DIFdel{respectively. This is a calibration
failure with an immediate operational consequence, and it is invisible in
accuracy, $F_{1}$ or AUC.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \begin{table}[t]
%DIFDELCMD < \caption{Decision threshold required to reach a usable operational
%DIFDELCMD < precision, and its cost in recall and alert volume.}
%DIFDELCMD < \label{tab:m_ppvcross}
%DIFDELCMD < \centering
%DIFDELCMD < %%%
%DIF <  GENERATED by analysis/make_tables.py on 2026-09-21
%DIF <  source: results\EXP-004\processed\ppv_crossings_radio.csv
%DIF <  commit: e45aa5b
%DIF <  DO NOT EDIT. Edit the generator, then re-run `make tables`.

chosen point inside that range makes the verdict an artefact of the choice. Table~\ref{tab:m_latency} and Fig.~\ref{fig:m_latency} therefore report, for each architecture, the smallest budget its 99th percentile conforms to.

than of the measurement platform. }%DIFDELCMD <

do. One set of measurements thus supports four different conclusions,

\section{\DIFdel{Comparative Analysis}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{section}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < \label{sec:comparative}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\subsection{\DIFdel{Against the Reported State of the Art}}

%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{subsection}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{Direct numerical comparison with published O-RAN intrusion detection results is not meaningful, because those results are in-distribution scores on corpora we do not share, and our own in-distribution scores in Table~\ref{tab:indist} are in the same range. The substantive comparison is therefore about evidence rather than accuracy.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{We surveyed reporting practice using the following protocol. We queried IEEE Xplore, the ACM Digital Library and Scopus for records published between January 2021 and December 2025 matching (\texttt{"O-RAN" OR "Open RAN" OR "5G"}) \texttt{AND} (\texttt{"intrusion detection" OR "anomaly detection"}) \texttt{AND} (\texttt{"machine learning" OR "deep learning"}), restricted to peer-reviewed conference and journal papers in English. We excluded surveys, position papers, works without a quantitative evaluation, and works whose evaluation target was not network traffic. Two authors screened titles and abstracts independently and resolved disagreements by discussion, then assessed the full text of retained records against the eight reporting properties of Table~\ref{tab:survey}. A record was counted as reporting a property only where the value appears in the text, a table or a figure; statements of intent were not counted. Table~\ref{tab:survey} gives the resulting counts. Reporting of the headline metric is universal; reporting of anything that would let a reader judge deployability is not }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Table~\ref{tab:predicate} gives the result. No architecture passes: \PredPass{} of six. Every architecture fails the generalization term, with upper bounds of \PredGenMin{} to \PredGenMax{}, and every architecture fails the alert term by a wide margin: at recall of at least 0.5, the best operational precision on }\DB{} \DIFadd{is \PredPPVmax{} and the fewest false alerts per hour is \PredFAmin{}. The verdict is therefore insensitive to the bounds chosen: it holds for any $\rho$ above \PredPPVmax{}, any $A_{\max}$ below \PredFAmin{} per hour and any $\delta$ below \PredGenMin{}, and it does not depend on the latency term at all. It also survives the label conflicts of Section~\ref{sec:res:target}: evaluated on }\DB{} \DIFadd{without the benign copies, \PredCleanPass{} of six pass, the best operational precision at recall of at least 0.5 is \PredCleanPPVmax{}, and the fewest false alerts per hour are \PredCleanFAmin. That term matters only once the first two pass; on our emulated measurements it would not decide the verdict}\DIFaddend .

%DIF <  executable; run it and substitute the resulting counts before submission.

\DIFdelbegin \subsection{\DIFdel{Applying the Deployability Predicate}}

%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{subsection}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The three bounds in \eqref{eq:deploy} require justification rather than assertion. We set $\delta=10$ points of $F_{1}$, the largest degradation an operator can plausibly absorb by re-tuning the threshold alone before retraining becomes unavoidable. We derive $A_{\max}$ from analyst capacity rather than choosing it: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Does the test discriminate?} A test that no detector passes could simply be too strict. The published 5G-NIDD pipeline of Section~\ref{sec:res:target} shows that it is not. On its own random split, with the test's alert term evaluated on that split, \PubRzPassN{} of its five classifiers pass: operational precision reaches \PubRzPPVmax{} at $\pi = 0.002$ with as few as \PubRzFAmin{} false alerts per hour }\DIFaddend at \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a triage rate of twelve alerts per analyst-hour and three analysts on shift, a tier-one queue absorbs thirty-six escalations per hour , and after an automation pass the sustainable raw rate is of order two hundred per hour. $B=10$\,ms follows from the specification \cite{oran_wg3_ricarch} and is not ours to choose.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{$\lambda_b = 240{,}000$. The same pipeline fails the test one rung later, when the two position counters are removed, and at every later rung, on all flows and on the flows without the benign copies (Table~\ref{tab:ladder}). The test separates an evaluation that predicts deployment from one that does not; the detectors of this paper fail it because their evaluations are of the second kind.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Instantiating \eqref{eq:deploy} with these bounds yields $\mathcal{P}(f_{\theta})=0$ for \emph{every} architecture we evaluated, and each fails for a different reason. The decision tree and logistic regression satisfy the latency term and fail the generalisation and alert terms. The random forest attains the best target score of the six and misses the latency term by more than an order of magnitude. The MLP has the best held-out source score in the study and fails the generalisation term absolutely, transferring to below a stratified coin flip.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \subsection{\DIFadd{Relation to Reported Results}}

\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{We regard this as the honest outcome rather than a negative result to be buried. Reporting only Table~\ref{tab:indist} }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Published O-RAN and 5G intrusion detection results are mostly in-distribution scores on corpora we do not share, and our own in-distribution scores under a random split are high as well (Table~\ref{tab:leakage}). A direct numerical comparison would therefore compare protocols rather than detectors. What the results above allow is a statement about which reported quantities predict deployment: held-out macro-$F_1$ }\DIFaddend under a random split \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{would have supported a claim of a deployable high-accuracy detection xApp, and that claim would have been wrong in three independent ways.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{One caveat belongs with the predicate rather than in the limitations, because it governs how \eqref{eq:deploy} should be read. The third term is evaluated on \emph{emulated} latency, and the emulation is a Python prototype rather than a deployed implementation. A published measurement inside a real Near-RT RIC reports inference three orders of magnitude faster for the same model families~\cite{obiuwevwi2026realtime}. The latency term therefore discriminates between our implementations, not between the architectures, and it should be re-evaluated against any deployed system before it is used to exclude a candidate}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{does not, and the quantities in the reporting requirements of Section~\ref{sec:conclusion} are those that changed the conclusions here}\DIFaddend .

\section{Reproducibility and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Ethical Considerations}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Ethics}\DIFaddend }
\label{sec:repro}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{We release the feature-harmonisation code, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Code, configuration, the seeded split procedures from which every split is regenerated deterministically, raw per-seed results, and }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{split manifests giving the exact device-level partition of }%DIFDELCMD < \DA{}%%%
\DIFdel{, the five random seeds, the trained model artefacts in ONNX form, the xApp container image referenced by digest, and the replay harness used for the latency measurements. Package versions are pinned as stated in Section~\ref{sec:setup}, and every tablein this paper is regenerated from raw measurement logs by a single script, so that the reported figures cannot drift from the underlying data. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{generators for every table, figure and in-text number are available at }\url{https://github.com/adeliusa486/oran-ids-deployability}\DIFadd{. Both corpora are public: }\DA{} \DIFadd{under CC-BY-4.0 from its authors~\cite{abedzadeh2025netslab} and }\DIFaddend \DB{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is publicly available }\DIFdelend from its authors\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\cite{samarakoon2022nidd}; }%DIFDELCMD < \DA{} %%%
\DIFdel{is released under the same terms as the testbed platform on which it was collected. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The traffic analysed here originates from instrumented }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{~\cite{samarakoon2022nidd}. The traffic comes from }\DIFaddend testbed devices and a \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{controlled }\DIFdelend test network, not from \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a production network carrying subscriber traffic}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{subscribers}\DIFaddend , so no personal data \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{or subscriber identifiers were processed. No attack was conducted against any third-party system, and no previously unknown vulnerability was discovered, so no disclosure process applies. We note that publishing the degradation characteristics of a detector }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{was processed. Publishing where detectors fail }\DIFaddend carries a modest dual-use risk\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, in that an adversary could infer which conditions weaken it}\DIFdelend ; we judge \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{this }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{it }\DIFaddend outweighed by the benefit to operators of knowing that \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{published accuracy figures do not transfer, which is the premise on which such systems are currently being procured}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{reported accuracy does not transfer}\DIFaddend .

\section{Limitations}
\label{sec:limitations}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Several limitations qualify these findings, and the first of them bounds everything else.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\textbf{The exporter confound is not controlled.} }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Exporter.} }\DIFaddend \DA{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is exported by Zeek }\DIFdelend and \DB{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{by Argus, and the two do not agree about where a flow ends --- median flow duration differs by four orders of magnitude between them. Re-extracting both corpora with a single exporterwas ruled out on cost grounds, so every $\Delta_{F_{1}}$ we report is an \emph{upper bound} spanning an independently collected deployment \emph{and} an independent feature-extraction pipeline. It must not be read as a measurement of deployment shift alone. The magnitude of the confound is not merely acknowledged but quantified, by others: a classifier predicting which corpus a flow came from reaches 0.993 balanced accuracy on harmonised features of these two corpora~\cite{abraheem2026bidirectional}}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{were exported by different tools, and we could not re-extract either corpus with a common exporter: our copy of }\DB{} \DIFadd{contains Argus flow records only, and no Zeek installation was available on the measurement host. The domain classifier and the robust subset show that the corpora differ in traffic composition as well as in flow segmentation, but they do not measure how much of the transfer gap a common exporter would remove. The base-station split inside }\DB{} \DIFadd{(Section~\ref{sec:res:target}) changes the site while keeping the exporter, which bounds part of the question from one side}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\textbf{All latency figures are emulated.} They were taken on a Windows host with no CPU isolation, in a single Python process, with no RIC in the path. They support relative ordering and stage decomposition, and they support \emph{no} conformance claim. A real measurement on OpenAirInterface and FlexRIC reports inference three orders of magnitude faster for the same model families~\cite{obiuwevwi2026realtime}, which is why we attribute the near-real-time crossing point to the implementation rather than to the architecture. CPU and memory under load are not measured at all}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Target labels.} Of }\DB{}\DIFadd{'s benign flows, \LcBenignTwinPct\% are exact copies of records labeled as UDP flood elsewhere in the corpus (Section~\ref{sec:res:target}). We cannot tell from the published files which label is correct, so every }\DB{} \DIFadd{operating point is reported with and without these flows. The assignment of the two capture passes to the two base stations is inferred from the repeated capture schedule and the restarted row index; the dataset does not state it}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\textbf{The grouping key is confounded with attack identity, and unequally across layers.} Grouping the radio layer by capture sessionis perfectly aligned with attack category --- purity $1.000$ --- because each run exercises one scenario, so no radio-layer result can distinguish memorising a run from memorising an attack }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Radio capture schedule.} Nine of the ten benign radio sessions were recorded six weeks before any attack session, mostly with one UE, and every radio label is a session label. A radio detector may therefore separate capture periods as well as attacks. The one benign session from the attack period is flagged almost entirely, but so are two early sessions (Table~\ref{tab:benign}), and ten benign sessions cannot separate the two explanations}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Network-layer grouping by source address reaches only $20.6\%$ of an oracle that groups directly by attack type, so the two are separable there. A run-disjoint label should not be quoted without the corresponding purity figure.

}\DIFdelend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\textbf{The effective sample size is smaller than it appears.} Twenty split seeds resample thirty capture sessions ; they do not enlarge that population. Intervals are paired $t$-intervals over split means rather than bootstraps for this reason, and the temporal analysis is powered to detect a large effect and nothing subtler.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\textbf{Three attack categories cannot be tested in transfer.} }%DIFDELCMD < \DB{} %%%
\DIFdel{contains no DDoS, brute-force or web attacks, so those categories are reported as untestable rather than averaged into a macro score as though they had been evaluated.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\textbf{Two corpora are two points.} They }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Two corpora.} Two corpora }\DIFaddend establish that a gap exists, not how it varies across \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the space of deployments. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{deployments. A third independently collected O-RAN corpus with paired radio telemetry was not available to us.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The operational prevalence $\pi=0.002$ in \eqref{eq:ppv} is a declared assumption, not a measurement. We therefore report the sweep rather than any single value, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Latency.} All latency figures are emulated on one Windows host without CPU isolation, }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{we note that PPV depends on $\pi$ while alert volume scales linearly and independently in the benign arrival rate, so the two-dimensional parameter space collapses to one dimension plus a multiplier. The prior correction of \eqref{eq:prior} assumes $\pi$ is known, which in deployment it is not ; where we apply it with a known target prior we label the result an oracle and draw no claim from it.

}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{$t_{\mathrm{ind}}$, $t_{\mathrm{act}}$ and $t_{q}$ are not measured. CPU and memory use under load are not measured.

}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Finally, we evaluate supervised binary detectiononly. Unsupervised baselines such as isolation forests \cite{liu2008isolation}, feature-attribution analysis \cite{lundberg2017shap} and multi-class attack attribution are left to future work, and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Sample size.} The radio layer has 30 capture sessions, each with a single scenario, so session and attack scenario are confounded, and }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{last of these would likely transfer worse still}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{protocol and temporal analyses can detect large effects only. With ten benign sessions, the radio layer's false positive rate, and with it its operational precision, carries a wide interval (Section~\ref{sec:res:alerts}). The transfer statistics vary training sets over 20 seeds but evaluate one fixed target corpus, so they do not describe other deployments}\DIFaddend .

\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\emph{Scope.} We evaluate binary detection. Multi-class attribution, feature attribution~\cite{lundberg2017shap} and the fusion of radio and flow features are left open; the published summary files cannot be joined record by record, and fusion requires the raw archives, as in~\cite{fard2026crosslayer}; three attack categories of }\DA{} \DIFadd{are absent from }\DB{} \DIFadd{and can be tested in one direction only.

}

\DIFaddend \section{Conclusion and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Future Work}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Reporting Requirements}\DIFaddend }
\label{sec:conclusion}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{An intrusion detection xApp evaluated the usual way tells you very little about how it will behave anywhere else. On an independently collected deployment, every architecture we tested degrades significantly, the best of them clears a stratified coin flip by }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.148} %%%
\DIFdel{of macro-$F_{1}$, and the architecture that scored \emph{highest} on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{A }\DIFaddend held-out \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{source data falls \emph{below} that floor, with a balanced accuracy of }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.4966}%%%
\DIFdel{. Across six architectures there is no significant rank correlation between in-distribution and transfer performance. At the declared deployment base rate the same detectors carry corpusprecision near }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.93} %%%
\DIFdel{and pooled operational precision between }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0055} %%%
\DIFdel{and }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{0.0077}%%%
\DIFdel{, a gap of more than two orders of magnitude, while emitting tens to hundreds of thousands of mostly-false alerts per hour.

}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{None of this is visible in the number a paper usually reports. That is the argument of this work}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{score on one corpus says little about a detection xApp's behavior elsewhere. On the corpora studied here, a random split inflates the score because models recognize capture sessions; the radio corpus's capture schedule leaves no room to estimate drift}\DIFaddend , and the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{strongest evidence for it is that the argument applies to us. Two findings we previously published did not survive re-examination during this study. A reported sixfold spread in deployment precision between architectures was an artefact of averaging a non-linear functional across evaluation folds rather than pooling counts; pooled, the spread is }%DIFDELCMD < \measured{1.40}%%%
\DIFdel{. A methodological caveat that had been carried forward through three phases, and had determined which measurement layer the work relied on, turned out on measurement to apply more strongly to the layer it had been used to protect. We report both corrections here rather than in an erratum, because a paper arguing that evaluation practice produces misleading numbers should be judged first on its own. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{benign session a detector meets sets its false positive rate; a published 99.9\% on the target corpus rests on record-position fields, and half of that corpus carries labels its records cannot determine. On flows with consistent labels, detectors transferred from the other corpus reach balanced accuracy \TrCleanTgtBAmin{} to \TrCleanTgtBAmax{}, below what the target allows, and at a realistic base rate no threshold yields usable operational precision. }\DIFaddend We therefore propose that papers claiming a deployable O-RAN detection capability report \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, at minimum}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at least}\DIFaddend : (i) performance on \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{at least one corpus not used for training or model selection, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{a corpus }\DIFaddend collected independently of the training \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{data, in a prevalence-invariant metric such as balanced accuracy or ROC-AUC, beside input-blind baselines and a reference trained inside that }\DIFaddend corpus; (ii) the \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{split protocol and its group key, results under a group-disjoint split, and the category coverage of any time-based split; (iii) the }\DIFaddend false positive rate \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{measured on benign-only traffic, together with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{on benign traffic, with its unit, }\DIFaddend the resulting alert rate\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{per unit time }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend and the operational precision \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{implied by a stated attack base rate; (iii) the 99th-percentile decision latency under a stated offered load, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at a stated base rate, all from pooled confusion counts with intervals that resample clusters; (iv) the operating threshold, how it was chosen, and the recall it gives up; (v) intervals that account for overlap between resampled splits; (vi) tail latency with its stages, implementation and host, }\DIFaddend measured on a real RIC \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than in isolation, alongside the container CPU and memory footprint; (iv) the operating threshold actually }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{before any conformance claim is made; and (vii) evidence that the labels are a function of the features }\DIFaddend used, with \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the recall it sacrifices, rather than an implicit $\tau=0.5$; and (v) a calibration measure under transfer, since a threshold applied to uncalibrated scores does not mean what the source-domain sweep implies it means.}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{no identical records under opposite labels, and that no record-position or identifier field is a feature.}\bibliographystyle{IEEEtran}
